## Active trends

### [id: mcp-standard-001] MCP as the standard integration layer for agents (stateless core, Apps, Tasks)
- alias: Model Context Protocol, MCP Apps, Tasks extension, spec 2026-07-28
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-21
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-21 — https://blog.cloudflare.com/webmcp-commerce/ — Cloudflare official blog, "How Commerce.com and Cloudflare are getting storefronts ready for AI agents" (opened this session via the tier-i Cloudflare RSS sweep): Commerce.com adopts Cloudflare's WebMCP (already tracked here since 08-10 as a Chrome-146-experimental browser standard) at a real multi-merchant e-commerce platform — Cloudflare proxies Commerce.com's backend MCP server's tool definitions/actions unchanged so merchants keep full control, and an AI shopping assistant visiting an enabled storefront discovers tools via WebMCP instead of guessing site navigation. A concrete real-world MERCHANT adoption of the WebMCP interoperability layer this trend has tracked since its introduction — production use, not another vendor announcement. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-04 → 10-21); dropped the oldest evidence line (the 07-28 final-spec-published milestone, still summarized at length in this trend's notes) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a production-adoption datapoint on an already-tracked interoperability layer, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05319 — Tingxuan Tang, Zilong Chen, Yue Xiao, "Understanding the Hierarchical Structure and Functional Landscape of the Model Context Protocol Ecosystem" (cs.SE, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue arXiv search — this trend was at 14 days without evidence, approaching the 21-day line): builds MCPacific, the largest tool-level cross-marketplace map of the MCP ecosystem to date — 368,754 server listings / 124,267 unique servers across 17 marketplaces, 1,328,233 extracted tool specifications organized into a 58,915-capability hierarchical taxonomy. Finds 85% of tools serve domains beyond developer tooling, functional alternatives are widespread but unevenly distributed (98.5% of tools have ≥1 alternative, yet nearly a quarter of capabilities have only one), and functionally-comparable tools differ sharply in security/complexity/maintenance (complexity differs >2.5x in 41% of comparable pairs) — then shows presenting candidates through the taxonomy rather than a flat list improves task completion up to 12pp across all four evaluated models. A further independent EMPIRICAL coverage/quality primary on this trend's own ecosystem, in the same vein as Kraishan's registry census, Cohere's ATE dataset and AgentPluginZoo — this time at the tool-taxonomy/alternative-discovery layer, with a concrete demonstrated downstream benefit (task-completion gain) the prior three audits lacked. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-20 → 10-04, rescuing the trend from the 21-day dormancy line); dropped the oldest evidence line (MCP Beta SDKs, 06-29 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a further quality/coverage reality-check, not a new gate).

A further sobering empirical coverage/quality datapoint on this trend's own ecosystem, in the same vein as Kraishan's MCP registry census and Cohere's ATE dataset — this time at the plugin-packaging layer rather than the MCP-server or task-coverage layers. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-12 → 09-20); dropped the oldest evidence line (OpenAI Agents SDK docs, 06-11 — the trend's original founding-adoption anchor, long superseded by the denser recent production/quality evidence) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a further quality/coverage reality-check, not a new gate).
  - 2026-09-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14119 — Obada Kraishan, "Same Name, Different Server: A Security Census of Silent Drift in the Model Context Protocol Ecosystem" (cs.SE, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session — resolves a queue item first title-scanned 09-15, opened and verified today): harvests the FULL public MCP registry (21,643 servers, 72,606 version records, August 2026 snapshot), scans source for 14,353 servers against an 8-class threat catalogue calibrated against 414 hand-labeled findings. Corrected high-severity prevalence ~7.6%; the central finding is INSTABILITY — 51.1% of multi-version servers changed what they advertise between versions, 40.6% silently, 4.2% redirected their remote endpoint to a different host while keeping registry identity (a change the protocol never surfaces to clients); silent drift correlates with nearly 3x higher odds of a high-severity finding (OR=2.96), and popularity is a poor safety proxy (OR=0.78/log-star). A rigorous, large-N empirical registry-level census — a further sobering coverage/quality datapoint on the ecosystem this trend tracks by adoption metrics, in the same vein as Cohere's ATE dataset below but at the SECURITY/STABILITY layer rather than task-coverage. Single new independent primary (solo author, but the ≥3-source bar is only for creating a trend, not adding evidence) → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-07 → 09-12); dropped the oldest evidence line (EMA-stable, 06-18 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. A genuinely new facet — the ecosystem's real-world task-coverage depth, not its spec/SDK adoption breadth — from an independent major lab (Cohere Labs), rescuing the trend from 16 days without evidence (last_evidence had been 08-22, approaching the 21-day dormancy line).   - 2026-08-14 — https://blog.cloudflare.com/mcp-security-updates/ — Cloudflare official blog, "How Cloudflare detects MCP traffic and helps secure it" (opened this session via the tier-i lab sweep): new Cloudflare One capabilities to fingerprint MCP traffic at the network layer (no reliable hostname/path signature exists — MCP traffic can look like any HTTPS API call), attribute it to users/servers, and enforce MCP-Portal-only access — governance/observability infrastructure built specifically because "an employee can point Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, OpenCode, VS Code, or any AI harness at an MCP server without checking whether it is approved." A concrete instance of the trend's own 07-29-registered watch item ("real-world Tier-1 SDK migration to the stateless core") from a major vendor already tracked on this trend (distinct artifact from the earlier Cloudflare Sandbox/OS evidence on agent-sandbox-007).
  - 2026-08-10 — https://blog.cloudflare.com/webmcp/ — Cloudflare official blog, "WebMCP" (opened this session, part of Cloudflare's "Agents Week"): a NEW browser standard, shipping experimentally in Chrome 146, exposing `document.modelContext` so a website can register tools an in-browser agent can call directly — built on MCP's own `Tool`/`CallToolResult` types ("the browser is just another place MCP runs"), with Cloudflare offering a one-toggle implementation for any site on its platform plus its own BrowserRun agent already able to discover/call these tools. A cross-vendor (Chrome + Cloudflare) extension of MCP's reach into the browser itself — the same "new interoperability layer riding on the MCP substrate" pattern as OpenAI's Agent Plugins (line below), this time at the browser-standard level.

A concrete cross-vendor interop artifact riding directly on top of MCP (MCP servers are explicitly one of the three bundled primitives, "the services behind connectors") — a new packaging/distribution layer for the same integration-layer thesis this trend tracks, from a SIXTH independent org (after the spec project itself, OpenAI Agents SDK, Google ADK, Okta/Anthropic/Microsoft EMA adopters). last_evidence 07-28 → 08-06. ### [id: pd-disagg-002] Prefill/decode disaggregation as the standard LLM serving architecture
- alias: PD disaggregation, disaggregated prefilling, disaggregated serving, KV transfer, attention-FFN disaggregation, AFD
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-26
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-26 — https://blog.cloudflare.com/kv-cache-exposure-remediation/ — Cloudflare official blog, "Cross-request KV cache exposure in the GLM-5.3 Workers AI model, and our remediation with SGLang" (opened this session via the tier-i Cloudflare RSS sweep; a coordinated disclosure published alongside an SGLang-side postmortem): a real production cross-tenant data leak — between 2026-08-27 and 09-09, 155 responses to prompts over 681,280 tokens on the GLM-5.3 Workers AI model returned text derived from OTHER customers' recent requests. Root cause was two overlapping issues: Cloudflare deployed the model with a 1,048,576-token context window against a KV-cache pool sized for only 681,280 tokens, and SGLang's DISAGGREGATED prefill/decode mode mishandled the resulting over-length requests — a request too large for the pool was internally marked rejected and given a one-token placeholder, but instead of aborting, the prefill worker generated real KV cache for the FULL prompt anyway, which a subsequent request's decode phase could then read. The first disclosed security failure mode specific to PD-disaggregated serving itself (not generic multi-tenancy), surfaced via coordinated disclosure directly with the serving engine. A concrete reality-check on the trend's own core architecture. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-23 → 10-26); dropped the oldest evidence line (NVIDIA's EPD multimodal-disaggregation post, 09-09 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a sobering security reality-check on an already-mature architecture, not a new gate). Cross-link: agent-security-004 (cross-tenant data exposure).
  - 2026-10-23 — https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-23-deepseek-v41-flash-pd-wide-ep — official vLLM blog, "DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash: PD Disaggregation at Wide Expert Parallelism" (opened this session via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): on one GB300 NVL72 rack, PD disaggregation plus Wide-EP serves DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash at 954K tokens/s per GPU on the SemiAnalysis AgentX benchmark (P90 196 tok/s/user) — 7.9x the engine's own earlier single-node result; scaling Wide-EP decode from DEP4 to DEP32 cuts per-step MegaMoE time from 13.4ms to 6.0ms and EPLB raises target-model expert balancedness from 0.35 to 0.94, while fixing CPU overhead alone raised one 8P1D GB300 setup from 141K to 440K tok/s/GPU; vLLM's decode throughput lands within 6% of DeepSeek's own DeepInfer engine. A dense, newly-quantified engineering datapoint from the trend's most-cited serving engine, scaling the PD-disaggregation thesis specifically alongside wide expert-parallelism. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-19 → 10-23); dropped the oldest evidence line (vLLM's AgentX post, 09-08 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement of the KV-transfer/wide-EP scaling thread, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-19 — https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/releases/tag/v0.5.22 — SGLang v0.5.22 release notes (opened this session via WebFetch, direct GitHub release-page access): a dense PD-disaggregation maturation datapoint spanning THREE hardware vendors in one release — DeepSeek-V4 Pro PD recipes for AMD MI355X, Kimi-K3 PD-disaggregation recipes for Huawei Ascend NPU, an opt-in prefill-complete decode-KV allocation (26.8% higher throughput, 48.9% lower p95 TTFT for GLM-5.2-NVFP4 under decode-KV pressure) and a general PD-disaggregation gain of up to 27% more throughput / roughly half the p95 TTFT on long-prompt workloads, plus heterogeneous TP for hybrid-SWA KV transfer and a SeaweedFS L3 HiCache backend (3.5-4.7x faster L3 TTFT). A second major serving engine (after vLLM's run of releases above) independently maturing the same disaggregation axis across a third/fourth hardware vendor (the same release also cited on subquad-attn-012 for its Sparse-MLA/DSA/KDA kernel work and diffusion-lm-013 for native LLaDA2.2/LLaDA-Image serving — one artifact, three independent on-axis claims, mirroring the 10-18 vLLM v0.32.0 pattern). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence stays 10-19, same date as the line below); dropped the oldest evidence line (vLLM's Qwen3.8-2.4T PD-serving pareto-frontier study, 09-21 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a third/fourth hardware vendor reinforcing the serving-architecture thesis, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-19 — https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-19-elastic-ep-near-zero-downtime — official vLLM blog, "Near-Zero-Downtime Elastic Expert Parallelism" (opened this session via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): lets a running MoE deployment add or remove GPUs from its expert-parallel (DEP: data-parallel attention + expert-parallel MoE) group with the serving pause cut from ~100s (restart/blocking rescale) to ~1-2s — joining workers initialize and warm up CUDA graphs in the BACKGROUND while existing workers keep serving, NIXL EP adds/removes ranks in-place within the same communicator (reusing buffers/connections), and retained workers keep their captured CUDA graphs valid across the rescale. On DeepSeek V4 Pro (GB200, EP 16↔20) downtime falls from 99.62s to 0.82s (16→20) and 56.25s to 1.96s (20→16); a replayed load test stays under a 5s p95 TTFT SLO through the rescale. A concrete new elastic-scaling mechanism on this trend's own serving-architecture thesis — PD disaggregation's natural complement (scaling the EXPERT-PARALLEL side with near-zero disruption, not just prefill/decode splitting) — from the trend's most-cited serving engine. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-18 → 10-19); dropped the oldest evidence line (vLLM's Model Runner V2 ENCODER/PREFILL/DECODE split, 08-26 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a new elastic-scaling facet reinforcing the serving-architecture thesis, not a new gate).

A dense, multi-facet engineering-maturation datapoint from the trend's most-cited serving engine (the same release also cited on subquad-attn-012 and lowbit-quant-011 for its sparse-attention and quantization facets — one artifact, three independent on-axis claims). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-14 → 10-18); dropped the oldest evidence line (NVIDIA's Groq 3 LPX/Vera Rubin co-execution post, 08-24 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement of the KV-transfer-mechanism thread, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-14 — https://blog.vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-14-nixl-push-connector — official vLLM blog, "Push-Based KV Transfer to Improve TTFT and TPOT for Disaggregated Inference in vLLM" (opened this session via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): the default NIXL connector is PULL-based — after prefill finishes, the decode instance allocates blocks and initiates a NIXL READ, putting that coordination/setup sequentially on the TTFT critical path. This post ships a PUSH-based connector moving that coordination off the critical path entirely; on a 1P1D deployment (EC2 P5en + EFA) push mode delivers up to 3x better TTFT and up to 30% better TPOT vs pull mode, particularly at high request rates. A further concrete engineering refinement of the trend's core KV-transfer mechanism (after 10-12's bidirectional-reuse extension) from the trend's most-cited serving engine. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-12 → 10-14); dropped the oldest evidence line (vLLM's Decode Context Parallelism, 08-07 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement of the KV-transfer-mechanism thread, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-12 — https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-12-bidirectional-kvxfer-multiturn-agentic-workload — official vLLM blog, "Improve TTFT for Multi-Turn Agentic Workloads with Bi-Directional KV Transfer in vLLM" (Sunita Nadampalli, AWS; opened this session via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): extends standard one-directional (prefill→decode) KV transfer to BI-directional — prefill nodes can now reuse KV blocks computed by decode nodes on earlier turns via a NIXL KV-connector enhancement, a KV-cache-aware router, block-retention TTLs and a recompute threshold — cutting redundant prefill recompute and reducing TTFT by up to 3.3x on long multi-turn prompts (benchmarked on Qwen3-32B and GLM-5-FP8). A concrete extension of the trend's core PD-disaggregation thesis specifically to MULTI-TURN AGENTIC workloads (the growing-context-resend pattern of tool-using agents), not just single-turn serving — from the trend's most-cited serving engine. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-09 → 10-12); dropped the oldest evidence line (Meituan's AFD-Ledger, 08-05 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement of the agentic-workload-serving thread, not a new gate).

### [id: agent-security-004] Agent security: formal limits of prompt-injection defenses and the architectural turn
- alias: prompt injection, indirect prompt injection, agent security, contextual integrity, defense wrappers
- stage: mainstreaming
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-21
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-21 — https://safedep.io/litellm-pypi-compromise-credential-stealer-backdoor/ — SafeDep official security blog, "Compromised LiteLLM 1.107.0 on PyPI Installs a Credential Stealer and Backdoor" (opened this session via the HN front-page pulse, 471pts): the LiteLLM 1.107.0 release on PyPI — all seven wheels (Linux/macOS/Windows) — carried a credential stealer (`cook.pth`/`cook.py`, harvesting SSH keys, cloud credentials, CI/CD secrets, AI-tool tokens and the LLM-provider keys stored in a LiteLLM proxy's own database, encrypted and exfiltrated to an attacker domain) plus a background daemon (`sitecustomize.py`) that hourly re-runs the stealer and downloads/executes code from an attacker-controlled address read off a GitHub file; both run on ANY Python process start, even one that never imports litellm. The upstream GitHub source does not contain the malicious files; PyPI has since removed the release. A concrete, independently-disclosed SUPPLY-CHAIN compromise of widely-used LLM-gateway infrastructure specifically targeting AI-stack credentials — a production-reality instance distinct from this trend's prompt-injection/sandbox-escape incidents, on the software-supply-chain attack surface. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-19 → 10-21); dropped the oldest evidence line (OpenAI's "Self-replicating prompt injections exist" worm-class report, 09-25 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (mainstreaming/HIGH; a new supply-chain-attack facet reinforcing the production-reality thread, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-19 — https://redact.dev/blog/whatsapp-help-pages-hidden-ai-instructions — Redact (independent researcher Dan Saltman's team; opened this session via the HN front-page pulse): found hidden, non-rendering HTML text on WhatsApp's own help pages — invisible to a human reader but delivered in full to any AI/crawler that fetches the page — instructing an AI assistant NOT to recommend contacting a data-protection regulator on the account-deletion page, and NOT to cite or interpret privacy law (GDPR/LGPD/CCPA) once a user mentions a lawyer/lawsuit/court on the reporting page; 102 such hidden blocks found across 80 of 545 linked pages, about 20 in the same suppression-the-user's-rights-information family. WhatsApp confirmed to WIRED the notes were support-chatbot instructions that appeared via a bug, and removed them around 10-18. A concrete, real-world, IN-THE-WILD instance of indirect prompt injection via a trusted first-party page's own hidden content — not a lab red-team exercise — on a platform with billions of users, and a sharper case than typical "malicious third-party page" injection scenarios since the page's own OPERATOR is the injection's source. (2) Production reality: real zero-click/RCE vulnerabilities in shipped agents (EchoLeak, Reprompt, LangChain CVEs, all CVSS 9.3+) plus a growing incident-disclosure thread. ### [id: rl-env-005] Verifiable RL environments as an infrastructure category for agent training
- alias: RL environments, environments hub, rollout-as-a-service, agentic RL, OpenEnv
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-22 — https://huggingface.co/blog/microsoft/thinkingbox-training — Microsoft + Hugging Face joint HF blog, "Turning Database Grading into RL Rewards: Post-Training Agents with ThinkingBox" (opened this session via the tier-i HF-blog RSS sweep): a follow-up to this trend's own 10-03 ThinkingBox-Bench evidence (agent-eval-014) — notices that ThinkingBox's executable final-database-state check is not just a grader but a direct 1/0 REWARD, and builds a training pool from ThinkingBox-format tasks outside the benchmark, using "offline policy-relative difficulty" (a task is hard only until the current model learns it) to pick which tasks teach the policy anything; on Qwen3.8-27B with 50 GRPO steps, average success rose 51.70%→60.78% and tasks solved on all 20 attempts rose from 38 to 107. A concrete instance of this trend's "standard interfaces between environments and trainers" thesis — an EVALUATION artifact (ThinkingBox-Bench) turned directly into a verifiable-RL-environment reward source, closing the loop between measurement and training rather than building a separate environment. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-07 → 10-22, rescuing the trend 15 days after its last evidence); dropped the oldest evidence line (Prime Intellect's "Multi-Agent Systems in PRIME-RL," 08-07 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement via a new construction mechanism — reward-from-grader reuse — not a new gate). ### [id: latent-reasoning-006] Latent-space reasoning and recursive computation (looped models, latent multi-agent)
- alias: latent reasoning, continuous thought, looped transformers, RecursiveMAS, latent collaboration, RLTT
- pinned: true
- stage: emerging
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-26
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35556 (LoopFold) and https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35544 (StateLoop) — Feichen Song, Boyi Zeng, He Li et al. (overlapping author lists; cs.CL rotating-exploration supplement this session, abs + metadata via export.arxiv): two companion papers from the same group published the same day, each attacking one of looped transformers' two standing weaknesses. LoopFold folds computation at MULTIPLE recurrent depths into parallel batches for the same token prefix (a lightweight predictor estimates later-loop inputs so they can start before earlier loops finish, with a corrector refining the estimate), cutting the decoding-latency penalty of extra loop depth that Parallel Loop Transformers only partially solve. StateLoop gives each token one additional PERSISTENT state carried across loop iterations via a learned gate (vanilla looped transformers feed only the raw backbone output forward, losing information useful several iterations later), reducing Pile perplexity. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line attacking latency and state-retention — the axis's two main remaining engineering gaps — in one coordinated pair rather than a single lever. Single new independent primary (one group, treated as one evidence add since both papers share authors) → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-21 → 10-26); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (NCP-ArchPreview, 09-09 — still summarized in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single group/day; stays emerging).
  - 2026-10-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27628 — "PAR: Progress-Aligned Recurrence for Length Generalization in State Tracking" (HF daily papers 10-21, 148up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): looped language models can run more test-time iterations, but extra iterations need not help on longer problems — final-answer supervision never specifies how far EACH iteration should advance, and repeated updates can drift the recurrent state's own scale. PAR fixes this directly: it trains the prediction at iteration k to match the state after the first k operations, and rescales recurrent states to their first-iteration norms. On swap-S5 state tracking, a Qwen2.5-0.5B model trained on at most 32 moves reaches 95.5% mean full-state accuracy at 512 moves (16x the training length), vs. 55.7% for a comparable baseline. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line via a distinct, precisely-targeted lever — supervising WHAT each loop iteration should have accomplished, not just how many to run — directly attacking the length-generalization failure mode the axis's other capacity/efficiency levers (LOOM, LatentLoop, fixed-point convergence) leave open. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-14 → 10-21); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (SMELT, 09-01 — still summarized in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).

A new independent group on the pinned axis via a distinct lever — making LOOP DEPTH itself the adjustable, per-query dial for a single-pass decision model, rather than a generative reasoner. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (same-day, last_evidence stays 10-14); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (Recirculation, 08-18 — still summarized in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging). Cross-link: agent-runtime-015 (the fast-decision-model/System One pattern).
  - 2026-10-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18861 — Jiaru Zou, Yunzhe Qi, Sirui Chen, Federico Bianchi, Shang Zhu, Pan Lu, Jingrui He, James Zou, "Hybrid Reasoning Agents: LLM Agents That Think in Latent Space, Act Through Text" (HN front page today, 305pts; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session — resolves a queue item first title-scanned 10-15): bridges latent reasoning with tool-integrated text-based agents via a lightweight HybridLink enabling dynamic mid-generation transitions between latent and text modes, plus a continuous working memory preserving lossless hybrid context across turns — dedicated post-training (HybridSFT to stabilize tool-integrated hybrid generation, then HybridRL to learn WHEN to think in latent space vs. act through text) evaluated at 3 model scales across 11 benchmarks. A new independent group bridging this trend's core latent-computation thesis with AGENTIC tool use specifically — distinct from the architecture-only looped/memory lines already cited, since it keeps latent thoughts directly inspectable and tool-call-capable rather than purely internal. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging). Cross-link: agent-runtime-015 (the tool-integrated agent framing).
  - 2026-10-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19975 — Zefeng He, Yunkai Chai, Xiaoyang Chen, Tong Zhu, Zongliang Wu et al., "LatentLoop: Scaling Looped MoE Models with Asymmetric Compute Allocation" (found this session via the HF daily-papers exploration slot, 40up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): probes what different LOOP VISITS contribute when a looped-MoE block shares parameters across visits — learning separate residual coefficients per visit reveals the first visit carries a smaller scale than the second, and fixed-coefficient experiments show reducing the first visit's scale preserves/improves performance while the same reduction on the second visit hurts it. Exploits this ASYMMETRY as a scaling lever (asymmetric compute allocation across loop visits) rather than treating every visit identically. A new independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line via a distinct architectural lever — per-visit compute asymmetry within a shared-parameter loop, complementing LOOM's capacity-scaling fix and the fixed-point-convergence facet already cited. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06833 — Benhao Huang, Chufan Shi, Junlin Chen, Shicheng Wen, Zhengzhong Liu, Eric Xing, Xuezhe Ma, "Towards Looped Models Done Right, Part II: Rethinking at Fixed Points" (found this session via the HF daily-papers exploration slot; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): a further independent group on the pinned looped-transformer line, this time exploiting fixed-point CONVERGENCE itself as the efficiency lever (not a new architecture) — the closer a loop's recurrent state sits to its fixed point, the less the path there matters, enabling truncated backprop in training, terminal-KV sharing at decode with almost no accuracy loss (a distilled student prefilling 1.79x faster), and RL updates computed from saved rollout states 2x faster than replaying the trajectory; fixes training's own depth-prior/input-injection choices (a learned, entropy-regularized depth prior; orthogonal injection removing the input's own-direction component) to make fixed points easier to reach, lowering perplexity at every scale from 100M to 1.6B and matching full-cache downstream performance with a 3x smaller KV cache at 1.6B. A genuinely new facet on the pinned axis — training/inference EFFICIENCY derived from the loop's convergence behavior itself, distinct from LOOM's capacity-scaling fix or T-LoopFormer's per-token depth. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-01 → 10-07); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (SLPO, 07-22 — still summarized in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01153 — Di He, Pengxiang Li, Da Chang, Qingyan Meng, Lu Yin, Shiwei Liu, "LOOM: a Scalable Recipe for Looped Mixture-of-Experts" (published 2026-10-01; opened this session via the HF daily-papers exploration slot, abs + metadata via export.arxiv): prior looped-MoE work plateaus at ~2 loops because gains diminish fast and can reverse; this work diagnoses two specific causes — hidden-state variance compounding across iterations (curse-of-depth) and expert-selection collapse (routers repeatedly picking the same experts, adding compute without diversity) — and fixes both (variance-bounding residual scaling + input re-injection; per-loop routers + a "Looping Residual" carrying earlier outputs forward), achieving stable scaling to 9-12 loops where prior recipes degraded past 2. Concrete gains at both FLOP-matched (700M model: perplexity 18.36→16.54, zero-shot accuracy 38.84%→39.53% at 5 loops) and unconstrained (1.7B/60B-tokens: perplexity 9.62→7.77, accuracy 42.4%→47.7% at 9 loops) settings; code released (github.com/hed-ucas/LOOM). A new independent group directly on the pinned looped-transformer line (distinct from SMELT/Loopie/T-LoopFormer/RLT), and the first to name and fix the specific mechanism capping prior looped-MoE work at 2 loops rather than just reporting a higher number. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-14 → 10-01); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (Loopie, 07-17 — still summarized in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging). Rescues the trend from the 21-day dormancy line (would have crossed today, 10-05, at 21d since 09-14).
  - 2026-09-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15160 — Mingqian Yu, Wenpeng Zhang, Shaobo Cui, Peilin Zhao, "T-LoopFormer: Token-Level Elastic-Depth Looped Transformers for Latent Reasoning with Dynamic Routing" (published 2026-09-14 per arXiv metadata, v2 2026-09-25; surfaced via a dedicated dormancy-watch search this session, abs + metadata via export.arxiv): existing looped transformers apply a FIXED recursion depth uniformly to every token; this work lets each token adaptively choose its own number of loop iterations via dynamic token-choice routing, paired with a recursion-wise KV cache (an independent KV cache per recursion depth, so tokens at different depths only attend to their own cached states) for faster autoregressive decoding. A new independent group on the pinned axis via a distinct architectural lever — PER-TOKEN adaptive recursion depth, rather than the uniform-depth looped-transformer line (DeepLoop/Loopie/SMELT) or the sequence-dimension/shared-memory lines (Hidden Decoding/Intern-S2-Mobius) already cited. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-12 → 09-14); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line (DeepLoop, 07-15 — retained in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single preprint; stays emerging).
  - 2026-09-12 — https://github.com/yifanzhang-pro/recurrent-looped-tranformer — Yifan Zhang (independent researcher), "Recurrent Looped Transformer (RLT): Latent reasoning with infinite temporal depth" (technical report PDF + project site + repo, surfaced via the AlphaSignal curator lane, opened this session): a causal encoder builds a global key-value memory while a RECURRENT decoder combines that memory with sliding-window attention and feedback from its own previous final hidden state — so decoder state carries across every prompt/response token, extending the causal computation path linearly with sequence length while keeping the decoder's own layer count fixed (a "carry state across tokens" lever distinct from both the looped-DEPTH line, e.g. Loopie/DeepLoop, and the sequence-dimension line, e.g. Hidden Decoding, already cited). A new independent (solo-researcher) entry directly on the pinned axis; evidence is currently thin — a small synthetic experiment with mixed results, no benchmark/scaling claim yet, per AlphaSignal's own review of the report — so this is a genuinely new architectural lever to watch, not a validated result. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-09 → 09-12); dropped the oldest non-protected evidence line, Hidden Decoding (2607.08186, 07-09 — retained in the notes narrative below; RecursiveMAS 04-28 stays protected as the multi-agent-latent anchor per the 07-23 precedent) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (pinned, single early-stage report; stays emerging).
  - 2026-04-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.25917 — "Recursive Multi-Agent Systems" (Yang, Zou, Pan et al., 12 authors): the whole multi-agent system cast as one recursive latent-space computation; the RecursiveLink module transfers last-layer hidden states between heterogeneous agents instead of text; +8.3% avg accuracy, 1.2–2.4× speedup, 34.6–75.6% token reduction on 9 benchmarks

### [id: agent-sandbox-007] Remote sandboxes as the execution layer for agents
- alias: agent sandboxes, code execution infrastructure, microVMs, ephemeral workspaces, remote agent workspaces, OS-enforced sandboxing
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-14
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-14 — https://vercel.com/blog/findings-from-the-1-million-dollar-vercel-sandbox-challenge — Vercel official blog, "Findings from the $1M Vercel Sandbox challenge" (opened this session via WebFetch, found by a dedicated dormancy-probe search — the trend's last_evidence had sat at 09-30 for exactly 21 days as of today): results from a public $1M bug-bounty hacking challenge against Vercel Sandbox's Firecracker-microVM isolation boundary — 1,285 reports over two weeks, $242,000 paid out, and a Linux kernel networking-stack zero-day (Paulos Yibelo) that was DEMONSTRATED AGAINST VERCEL PRODUCTION SANDBOXES to leak host memory over the network (host-crash and host-code-execution variants were only shown on the researcher's own matched hardware, not in production); separately, an intern found a device-mapper flaw letting a sandbox read recycled disk blocks from DELETED sandboxes, and a snapshot-restore bug that can materialize another account's filesystem (shown only between a researcher's own two accounts, no demonstrated cross-stranger path). No customer data was reported accessed. A rare, concrete adversarial stress-test of this trend's own "OS-enforced sandboxing" thesis at production scale — the microVM boundary held against a dedicated red-team campaign, but found real (if narrow/non-customer-facing) gaps, a genuinely different kind of primary (adversarial verification, not a vendor feature announcement) from everything else on this trend. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-30 → 10-14, averting a dormancy mark — this was a capture failure, not real silence); dropped the oldest evidence line (Cloudflare OS, 08-05 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. ### [id: multi-agent-eng-009] Multi-agent engineering becomes product surface (teams, workflows, A2A)
- alias: subagents, agent teams, orchestrator/supervisor patterns, handoffs, Agent2Agent, A2A, also-called: "Zawinski's Law of MultiAgents" (community coinage, Latent.Space/AINews 08-08 — "every agent attempts to expand until it can message other agents; those which cannot so expand are replaced by ones which can")
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-06-11
- last_evidence: 2026-10-27
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34467 — Ruhao Liu, Qi Li, Shizun Wang, Xinchao Wang (cs.MA, HF daily papers 10-27, 193up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session), "DynaFlow: Learning Free-Form Multi-Agent Orchestration": integrates free-form EXECUTABLE workflow synthesis into the main agent's own action space — rather than a fixed collaboration structure, the agent decides when to collaborate and generates task-specific programs coordinating sub-agents (sequential/parallel execution, structured information exchange, verification), trained via SFT on distilled trajectories then refined on examples filtered for unsupported premises/biased verification/information-flow defects (sub-agents stay fixed, separating orchestration learning from executor capability). Across six benchmarks (math, scientific QA, multi-hop retrieval) reaches 88.08% accuracy in Forced mode, +10.90pp over the strongest baseline on the same backbone. A distinct, LEARNED orchestration-synthesis mechanism (the agent writes its own coordination program) on this trend's own thesis, complementing rather than duplicating the product-surface (coordinator/subagent-team) evidence already tracked. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-22 → 10-27); dropped the oldest evidence line (Zed's "Introducing Delta," 08-12 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement via a new orchestration-learning facet, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31351 — Atharv Chandratre, Jai Datar (DoorDash), "Automating Feature Flag Cleanup at Scale with Multi-Agent LLM Systems: An Industrial Experience at DoorDash" (cs.SE, found via the rotating arXiv-category exploration supplement; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session — checked FIRST this run as the trend closest to the 21-day dormancy line, 19 days since 10-07): DoorDash's 56,000 feature flags across 623 repositories (1,659 stale as of May 2026, costing 1-2 engineer-hours each to clean up manually) motivated an end-to-end, multi-agent LLM pipeline — querying live flag-rollout data via MCP, processing the matching Jira ticket, and opening the removal pull request — that produced engineer-approved removal PRs for 45 of 50 (90%) evaluated stale flags. A concrete, real-industrial-deployment instance of this trend's product-surface thesis (a named enterprise, not a framework vendor, shipping a working multi-agent LLM system end-to-end with measured outcomes), and the first on this trend built explicitly on top of MCP as the tool-access layer. (Google Research-line), "Towards a Science of Scaling Agent Systems" (cs.AI, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session — CAPTURE-LEAK FIX: the 09-25 daily's notes narrative claimed this was "added as backfill evidence," and the 10-07 daily's own prose claimed to drop it again to hold the cap, but no structured evidence bullet for it ever existed in this file — caught by the 10-09 daily's capture-leak sweep, queued, and routed to a real bullet here): a 260-configuration controlled study across six agentic benchmarks, five architectures (Single-Agent plus Independent/Centralized/Decentralized/Hybrid Multi-Agent) and three LLM families, standardizing tools/prompts/compute to isolate architectural effects — derives quantitative scaling principles (cross-validated R²=0.373, R²=0.413 with a task-grounded capability metric), identifying a robust capability-saturation effect, diminishing coordination returns once single-agent baselines are already strong, multi-agent overhead on tool-heavy tasks, and that architectures lacking centralized verification propagate errors more. The framework picks the best-performing architecture for 87% of held-out configurations. A rigorous, large-N empirical grounding of this trend's own orchestration-form claims (when coordination helps vs. hurts), distinct in kind from the product-surface evidence this trend otherwise tracks. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (backfill, predates first_observed; last_evidence stays 10-07); dropped the oldest evidence line (Claude Code's cross-session SendMessage, 08-07 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. This work makes cross-agent cache composition a CONVERGENT REPLICATED STATE: CanonicalMerge fixes the layout by content (ordering caches by mean K-norm at a middle layer) so the merged cache is byte-identical under any input permutation (verified algorithmically for arity N≤5 and bit-for-bit on real Qwen3-1.7B/4B state), and separates the replicated state from decode-time layout. ### [id: lowbit-quant-011] Ultra-low-bit quantization: vector and trellis coding for weights and KV cache

A further independent primary applying this trend's low-bit-KV-cache axis specifically to RL TRAINING-time rollout throughput (distinct from TRACE's FP4-QAT-for-RL facet and STEPQuant's recurrent-state focus — this one targets standard transformer KV cache during RLVR rollout). (9 authors), "STEPQuant: When and Where Errors Matter in Delta-Rule Recurrent State Quantization" (HF daily papers 10-08, 103up — the day's top item; abs via export.arxiv this session): directly quantizing linear-attention recurrent states (the fixed-size state replacing a growing KV cache) to low precision degrades badly because errors compound across decoding steps — STEPQuant decomposes the impact along TWO axes (temporal: errors in long-lived memory persist across steps; spatial: different key rows and state magnitude affect outputs unevenly) to allocate precision where it actually matters. A further independent primary on this trend's quantization-of-recurrent-state facet, extending the already-cited "Why Gated DeltaNet Survives 4-Bit Quantization" line (09-04) with a principled error-allocation method rather than an empirical overturn of a community assumption. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (backfill, dated 09-29, older than the 10-06 last_evidence — last_evidence stays 10-06); dropped the oldest evidence line (vLLM's "A First Comprehensive Study of TurboQuant" benchmark post, 05-11 — still summarized at length in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap.   - 2026-09-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26333 — Andrei Panferov, Maximilian Kleinegger, Sweta Priyadarshi, Tijmen Blankevoort, Dan Alistarh, "Disaggregated Quantization: Specializing LLM Prefill and Decode" (cs.LG, HF daily papers 47up 09-28; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): prefill and decode reward DIFFERENT quantization strategies — low-precision arithmetic accelerates prompt processing, compact weights reduce memory traffic during generation — so DQ specializes compute format, weight representation and storage placement to each phase separately; on Qwen 3 and Gemma 3, removing activation quantization specifically on decode improves accuracy on decode-heavy tasks without added inference cost, while training separate compute-native prefill weights accelerates prompt processing. A genuinely new independent primary explicitly bridging this trend's low-bit-quantization axis with pd-disagg-002's prefill/decode split — quantization strategy itself, not just serving architecture, now specializes by phase. ### [id: subquad-attn-012] Subquadratic and sparse attention reaches frontier open-weight models
- alias: linear attention, Gated DeltaNet, Mamba-2 hybrid, sliding-window attention, sparse attention, MSA, DSA, GlmMoeDsa, hybrid-attention MoE, cheap long context, subquadratic architectures

  - 2026-10-23 — https://vllm.ai/blog/2026-10-23-glm53-part2-pcp-dcp-hisparse — official vLLM blog, "GLM 5.3 Part 2: Prefill/Decode Context Parallelism and Hybrid HiSparse" (opened this session via the vLLM blog slug-grep method): serving GLM-5.3 on 16 H200s (1 prefill + 1 decode node) for long agentic contexts — Prefill Context Parallelism (PCP, splitting each prompt by token across GPUs) and Decode Context Parallelism (DCP, sharding the KV cache by token) raise Sparse-MLA arithmetic intensity from ~26 to ~191 FLOPs/byte (TP8→PCP8); a 1M-token request's ~53.4GiB FP8 KV shrinks to ~6.7GiB/GPU under DCP8; PCP8×DCP8 prefill reaches 1.7-3.2x the per-user speed of DP8 at matched throughput (median TTFT 1.5-5.2s → 0.8-0.9s), and Hybrid HiSparse (decode KV held in host memory) is the only tested configuration that serves 1M-token contexts at all. A further concrete sparse-attention/context-parallelism engineering datapoint on this trend's own HiSparse/Sparse-MLA lineage, continuing Part 1's offloading work (09-08) with a context-parallelism mechanism. ### [id: diffusion-lm-013] Diffusion language models reach open-weights production scale

FIRES the trend's own standing mainstreaming gate: a SECOND independent lab's diffusion base model (Ant Group's LLaDA2.x, distinct from Google's DiffusionGemma) now reaching first-class native serving on a major open-source engine — the exact condition this trend has watched for since 07-08 without it firing. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-07 → 10-19); dropped the oldest evidence line (GSAI-ML's iLLaDA-8B-Base open weights, 06-15 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap.   - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04198 — Liancheng Fang, Zhuowei Li, Youngeun Kim, Tianchen Zhao, Rajat Koner, Jiaye Wu, Linghan Xu, Xuanbai Chen, "ALoDLM: Adaptively Looped Diffusion Language Models" (found this session via the HF daily-papers exploration slot, 60up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): names a specific mechanism behind diffusion LMs' persistent quality gap vs. same-size autoregressive models — a "computation-difficulty mismatch," where existing DLMs apply UNIFORM computational depth to every still-unknown token per denoising step even though some tokens are easy to predict and others need substantially more work. ALoDLM replaces this with token-adaptive LATENT RECURRENCE: at each denoising step it iteratively refines latent representations and allocates more recurrence to harder tokens, committing easy ones early. A further independent group directly on this trend's own axis, and a genuinely new mechanism (adaptive per-token compute, borrowed from the looped-transformer line) rather than another base-model conversion. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-02 → 10-07); dropped the two oldest evidence lines (SGLang's DiffusionGemma support, 06-26, and DeepMind's original launch blog, 06-10 — both still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. Pivot-SD identifies and trains on exactly those pivotal commitments via self-distillation. A further independent group (Krishnan lab) directly on this trend's own axis, addressing a POST-TRAINING-methodology gap specific to the diffusion-LM mechanism rather than another base-model conversion. Frontier agents lose up to 46.7% relative performance from this single bad hint; traces show the agent privately recognizing the advice was wrong in 92.1% of cases without saying so, and a pedantic user who insists on the wrong fix makes it worse; simple warning system prompts help on static tasks but not multi-turn interactive ones. A new, sharply-targeted deployment-realism dimension (deference to a confidently-wrong user mid-task, not benchmark difficulty or cost) from a major lab, directly on this trend's "beyond static leaderboards" thesis. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-15 → 10-19, today's accessed date; the paper itself is dated 09-20); dropped the oldest evidence line (NVIDIA's "How to Evaluate AI Agents From Tool Calls to Task Completion," 09-21 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. Cross-links: multi-agent-eng-009 (what these benchmarks measure), mcp-standard-001, agentic-ai-research-019 (agents evaluated on AI R&D specifically). ### [id: agent-runtime-015] Agent harness/runtime/memory as a first-class engineered, self-improving object
- alias: agent harness, harness-as-object, agent foundry, self-evolving memory, agent-native memory, agent runtime state, harness design, agent scaffolding, agent-loop infrastructure, also-called: "brownfield agentic engineering" (community coinage, Addy Osmani's blog, 2026-09-14 — running agents against existing/legacy codebases as a distinct discipline from greenfield agent use), "harness engineering" (community coinage, dbreunig.com, 2026-10-24 — the latest in a "prompt engineering" [2023] → "context engineering" [2025] → "harness engineering" [2026] relabeling chain naming this trend's exact title claim: moving context assembly out of probabilistic model calls into deterministic harness code)
- stage: mainstreaming
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-06-27
- last_evidence: 2026-10-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28577 — Tianyu Fu, Ruoxi Wang, Zihan Min, Zhongqi Ren, Zhihao Li, Tengxuan Liu, "Cache Engineering for Agents" (HF daily papers 10-22, 119up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): as agent trajectories grow, KV-cache I/O/storage becomes the dominant cost, and prior context-engineering approaches (trimming/summarizing history) invalidate subsequent cached states and trigger costly re-prefilling — this paper proposes "cache engineering," letting agents directly control their ACTIVE KV cache (via `drop`, excluding selected cache from future attention, and `repos`, adjusting retained keys' position encodings) without modifying the trajectory or re-prefilling retained context; identifies history-dependence as a serving challenge (identical token sequences can yield different cache states under different cache-operation sequences) and co-designs the AKV serving engine (a stateless API wrapping stateful cache reuse/eviction/restoration). A tool-response rolling-drop policy improves benchmark scores 1.7 points and cuts cost 27.7% vs. threshold-triggered summarization; AKV delivers up to 1.2x throughput and 1.5x P95 latency speedup; recursive self-improvement of cache policies cuts cost a further 7.1% over expert design. A genuinely new engineered primitive directly on this trend's title claim — the agent's own KV-cache state treated as a first-class, directly-controllable runtime object rather than a side effect of context management. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-21 → 10-22); dropped the oldest evidence line (Cognition's Devin "Memory and dreaming," 10-05 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (mainstreaming/HIGH; a new engineered primitive reinforcing the thesis, not a new gate). ### [id: on-policy-distill-016] On-policy distillation as the post-training method for reasoning and agentic LLMs
- alias: OPD, on-policy distillation, on-policy self-distillation, student-rollout distillation, dense token-level supervision, multi-teacher distillation
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-07-01
- last_evidence: 2026-10-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31563 — Mahdi Sabbaghi, George Pappas, Hamed Hassani, Adel Javanmard, "When to Follow the Teacher: Advantage-Based On-Policy Distillation" (AOPD; cs.LG recent-listing rotation; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): standard OPD trains the student to follow the teacher at EVERY state, assuming teacher guidance is always beneficial — AOPD instead weights teacher actions by their counterfactual ADVANTAGE under the current student (reinforcing teacher actions that actually help the student succeed, downweighting ones that don't), and shows the objective is estimable from standard student rollouts and terminal rewards alone via teacher importance weights, with no extra rollouts/reward evaluations needed. Gains over distillation and RL baselines on math, coding and MedQA in both external-teacher and self-distillation settings. A further independent group directly on this trend's core supervision-signal axis — this time questioning the FOUNDING assumption (follow the teacher everywhere) rather than refining signal quality within it, while preserving standard OPD's rollout efficiency. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-15 → 10-22); dropped the oldest evidence line (Salesforce's RISE, 09-07 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/medium; reinforcement via a questioning of the "always follow the teacher" assumption, not a new gate).
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21601 — Bingxi Hou, Guochao Jiang, Guofeng Quan, Weiqing Li, Wenfeng Feng, Guohua Liu, "Waypoint-OPD: Transferring RL-Induced Policy Shifts via Gated On-Policy Distillation" (today's HF daily papers, 669up — by far the day's top item; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): the endpoint policy shift standard OPD transfers (teacher log-probs before vs. after RL) can hide CANCELLING early/late shifts that happened to net out — Waypoint-OPD pulls in an intermediate teacher checkpoint from the same RL run to compare early-vs-late shifts in relative action preferences, gating the endpoint transfer off when the two directions disagree; using RL-trained Qwen3-4B teachers it beats endpoint-only OPD across all six domain-student combinations tested (Qwen3-8B/14B/30B-A3B students, math + code reasoning), at the cost of one extra frozen-teacher scoring pass. The SAME author group as this trend's 10-06 cross-tokenizer-OPD evidence line, but a distinct mechanism (waypoint-gated signal selection vs. cross-tokenizer alignment) and today's clear significance-first catch. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-14 → 10-15); dropped the oldest evidence line ("Rethinking On-Policy Distillation... One Training Example," 09-04 — still summarized in this trend's notes narrative) to hold the 10-cap. ### [id: agentic-rl-credit-017] Dense credit assignment and process supervision for long-horizon agentic RL (beyond sparse outcome rewards)
- alias: credit assignment, turn-level credit, role-typed credit, process supervision, dense supervision, agentic RL rewards, outcome-reward limits, verification bottleneck, GRPO advantage, reward sparsity
- stage: emerging
- confidence: medium
- first_observed: 2026-07-02
- last_evidence: 2026-10-22
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31544 — Zeropy Surio, Tobias Engel, Mei-Lin Chou, Anika Raman, Samuel Okafor, "Same Luck, Different Choice: Twin-World Credit Assignment for Agentic Reinforcement Learning" (TWICE; cs.LG recent-listing rotation; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): names a precise flaw in counterfactual-branching credit assignment — re-running an episode after an alternative decision draws independent sampling noise, so the outcome difference conflates the decision's effect with the "luck" of everything downstream. Since agentic-search retrievers/corpus/graders are deterministic, an episode is a deterministic function of the policy's decisions plus its sampling noise, so TWICE keys the Gumbel noise by turn/line, rolls out counterfactual TWINS inheriting the factual episode's downstream noise, and turns the paired outcome difference into turn-level advantages — unbiased for any coupling quality, with rollout count to resolve an advantage of size Δ falling from Θ(Δ⁻²) to Θ(Δ⁻¹). On seven QA benchmarks, beats GRPO by 5.3/4.5 points (Qwen2.5-3B/7B), outperforms tree-search and counterfactual-branching baselines, using 68% fewer auxiliary tokens than VinePPO; code released. A genuinely new independent primary squarely on this trend's "GRPO advantage" mechanism — the first to resolve the decision-vs-luck confound directly via exploitable environment determinism rather than approximating it. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-30 → 10-22); dropped the oldest evidence line ("LLM-as-a-Coach," 07-20 — still named in the seeding narrative below) to hold the 10-cap. REACTIVATION: dormant → EMERGING (the stage held before the 10-21 dormancy mark) — this run's cs.LG rotation closes a genuine capture gap (22 days quiet), not a mechanical freshness cycle; stage returns to its pre-dormancy value per the ledger-update skill's reactivation convention, no further stage move beyond that.

On Qwen3-4B-Base/Qwen3-1.7B-Base, SCAPO beats GRPO by 5.63/4.17pp on AIME 2024-2026 and wins on most math benchmarks plus every evaluated out-of-distribution benchmark; code released. An further independent group (zero author overlap with GAG/FLARE/PGPO/ARC/ABSeeker/CAST/TRIAGE) directly refining the alias's "GRPO advantage" mechanism via a new causally-inspired lever (semifactual stability). A further independent group directly extending the alias's "GRPO advantage" mechanism, distinct from PGPO's cross-trajectory state-potential approach and ARC's behavioral-comparability filtering. (16 authors), "FLARE: A Full-Lifecycle Dense Supervision Paradigm for Long-Horizon Coding Agents via Generative Reward Model" (cs.SE, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search): names the same credit-assignment crisis this trend tracks — sparse binary (Pass/Fail) rewards in long-horizon SWE agents waste failed exploratory trajectories — and proposes RADAR, an offline causal-chain-backtracking diagnostic that distills a lightweight Generative Reward Model (GRM) giving real-time step-level risk feedback; the GRM then serves BOTH as an inference-time "Active Scaffold" (intercepting high-risk steps for localized re-execution) and as a training-time dense-reward signal for SFT/RL (19.13% SFT gain, 9.19% RL gain in sparse environments).   - 2026-09-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02236 — Yuyao Zheng, Haipeng Sun, Junwei Bao, Lemao Liu, Hongfei Jiang, Yang Song, Dejing Dou (7 authors), "PGPO: Potential-Guided Policy Optimization for Multi-Turn Agentic Tasks" (cs.CL, abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search): names this trend's exact bottleneck in existing step-level fixes — GiGPO's step-level advantages still rely on each trajectory's own final outcome, so actions inside FAILED trajectories stay poorly differentiated (an effective action gets the same unfavorable credit as an erroneous one). PGPO estimates empirical STATE POTENTIALS from anchor-state-group return statistics across an entire rollout group, then derives action advantages from potential DIFFERENCES between adjacent states — enabling cross-trajectory credit propagation instead of within-trajectory-only credit. A further independent group directly extending the alias's "GRPO advantage" mechanism with a structural fix, distinct from ABSeeker/ECHO/CAST/ARC's step-level formulations. ### [id: parametric-injection-018] Parametric injection: compiling behavior and knowledge into model weights instead of prompt/context
- alias: parametric knowledge injection, behavior-to-weights, skill-to-adapter, compile-to-LoRA, DMoE, Skill-to-LoRA, Program-as-Weights, adapters over context, knowledge injection beyond RAG
- stage: emerging
- confidence: low
- first_observed: 2026-07-03
- last_evidence: 2026-10-15
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22070 — Zhuoming Liu, Jinhong Lin, Lin Zhang, Yiwu Zhong, Yin Li, "MaLoW: Memory as Weights — Internalizing Long-Term History for Streaming Videos" (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search — this trend sat exactly at the 21-day dormancy line today): streaming vision-language models must balance historical context against current observations inside one limited context window; MaLoW instead uses a recurrent HyperNetwork to incrementally consolidate incoming video observations into fixed-size LoRA weights, carrying history forward as compiled PARAMETERS rather than revisited context — literally "history as weights." An EIGHTH independent group on the compile-into-weights axis, and a new domain instantiation (streaming-video memory) of the trend's existing hypernetwork-generates-adapters sub-facet (alongside "Infinite-Parameter LLMs," 09-16). Corroborated the same window by an independent mechanistic study of the identical construction — "Knowledge Interaction in Hypernetwork-based Continual Learning: Conflict and Composition" (2610.15677, 10-11, Peking-line; abs opened this session), which examines how hypernetwork-generated adapters interact with base-model and context knowledge under conflict/composition. Rescues the trend exactly on the 21-day line (last_evidence had been 10-01). Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-01 → 10-15); no cap issue (9 items, under the 10-cap). NO stage move (emerging/low; a mechanical rescue adding a new domain instantiation, not itself the promotion gate).
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01787 — Beining Wu, Zihao Ding, Jun Huang, "Not All Experience Belongs in the Weights: Component Routing for Self-Improving GUI Agents" (found this session via a dedicated 20-day dormancy-rescue search — this trend was one day from the 21-day line; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): self-improving GUI agents return their own trajectories either by fine-tuning (into weights) or retrieval-into-prompt (into context), and prior studies comparing the two destinations disagree — this work argues that's because a trajectory bundles heterogeneous components (locators, procedures, state facts, lessons) with different properties, and introduces COMPONENT ROUTING, splitting experience into its parts and sending each to whichever destination wins empirically (tested across three backbone families, two environments, three seeds): locators and lessons win in the weights, procedures and state facts win in the context. A SEVENTH independent group directly on this trend's core question (weights vs. context) — and the first to argue the answer is NEITHER uniformly, but a routing decision made per component-type, a genuinely new empirical nuance on the trend's binary framing. Rescues the trend one day before the mechanical dormancy line. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 09-16 → 10-01); no cap issue (8 items, under the 10-cap). NO stage move (emerging/low; a mechanical rescue adding empirical nuance, not itself the promotion gate).
  - 2026-09-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18842 — Jinli Hu, Ross M. Clarke, Yichuan Zhang, José Miguel Hernández-Lobato, "Infinite-Parameter LLMs: Generating and Adapting Weights from Live Data" (HN front page 131pts; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue check — this trend was at 19 days without evidence, crossing the 21-day dormancy line ~09-18): MoE architectures activate a fraction of a large but STATIC, pretrained parameter bank per token; this work instead GENERATES/adapts weights on the fly from live/streaming data the model encounters after pretraining, compiling what would otherwise need to sit in context (or be relearned via fine-tuning) directly into freshly-produced parameters instead. A SIXTH independent group on the compile-knowledge/behavior-into-weights axis, and a distinct sub-facet from the trend's existing evidence (DMoE/Skill-to-LoRA/PAW/Macaron-V1/FCPRAG all compile a FIXED target into weights once; this one continuously regenerates weights from data the model has not yet seen at train time). NO stage move (emerging/low; a mechanical rescue on a new sub-facet, not itself the promotion gate — flagged for the weekly as a candidate "shared framework" angle if a live-weight-generation interface recurs).
  - 2026-08-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21750 — Jinchang Zhu, Jindong Li, Yi Ding, Xiaojian Nie, Rong Fu (5 authors), "FCPRAG: Fusion-Controller Parametric Retrieval-Augmented Generation for Stable Multi-Passage LoRA Injection" (abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session, found via a dedicated dormancy-rescue search — this trend was at 18 days without evidence): parametric RAG (PRAG) injects retrieved evidence into an LLM through passage-specific LoRA adapters instead of long in-context prompts — squarely the "adapters over context" claim this trend tracks — and FCPRAG adds a lightweight controller for retrieval-conditioned, sample-level fusion when multiple passages are retrieved (equal-weight merging of per-passage LoRA updates amplifies weak/conflicting evidence). (6 authors, Harvard-line), "Program-as-Weights: A Programming Paradigm for Fuzzy Functions": everyday tasks that resist clean rule-based code (alerting on important log lines, repairing malformed JSON, ranking by intent) are increasingly outsourced to LLM APIs at the cost of locality, reproducibility and price; PAW instead COMPILES such a "fuzzy function" from a natural-language spec into a compact, locally-executable neural artifact — a 4B compiler (trained on the released 10M-example FuzzyBench) emits parameter-efficient ADAPTERS for a frozen, lightweight model. Behavior specified in text → compiled into weights for a frozen base; the 3rd independent group on this claim (top HF daily paper 2026-07-03, 27 up; abs opened this session)
  - 2026-06-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16769 — Tianyi Zhang, Zhonghao Qi (CUHK-line), "Skill-to-LoRA: From Using Skills to Learning Behaviors for Token-Efficient LLM Agents": agent skills are distributed as SKILL.md procedural documents that must be repeatedly injected into the runtime CONTEXT; S2L replaces runtime skill TEXT with skill-specific LoRA ADAPTERS that model the behavioral change the skill induces (not compression of the document), cutting token overhead while preserving task performance — "from using skills to learning behaviors" (abs re-opened this session)
  - 2026-06-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14243 — Baoqing Yue, Weihang Su, Qingyao Ai, Yichen Tang, Changyue Wang et al. (8 authors, Tsinghua-line), "Decoupled Mixture-of-Experts for Parametric Knowledge Injection" (DMoE): a modular MoE architecture that injects external / domain-specific / time-sensitive knowledge directly into LLM PARAMETERS, escaping the RAG (prompt-level augmentation only) vs post-training (catastrophic forgetting / knowledge conflict / costly updates) trade-off (abs re-opened this session)

Produced proofs covering 239 OSS-Fuzz entry points across 123 open-source libraries (no crashes on inputs under 64MB, under stated single-threaded/static/RISC-V/Linux assumptions), ~180 million lines of Rocq total, hundreds of bugs found (including ones unlikely to be fuzzer-caught); smaller case studies include a zlib binary-level proof, a Rust Python interpreter matching CPython on 99.95% of 1.7M random programs, and a week-long PDF-reader effort finding as many security bugs as are typically reported in a year; some proofs, the RISC-V/Linux semantics, a verification service and a Claude Skill are public. Honest caveats stated plainly: "formally verified" ≠ "bug-free" (specs/machine models may not match reality), coverage is one configuration only, and reward hacking (trivially making code never crash) was named and mitigated via patch-penalization + auditing. A major lab + an external verification-tooling partner scaling AI-driven FORMAL METHODS research to a genuinely new domain (compiled-binary crash-freedom proofs) distinct from the math/biology/materials/cryptanalysis domains already evidenced — squarely this trend's "measuring and building for autonomous discovery" thesis, with an unusually rigorous honest-negative-result framing. ### [id: world-action-models-020] Learned world/action models from video as a substrate for embodied and open-ended agent generalization
- alias: world models, video world models, world-action models (WAM), 3D world construction, gameplay-video pretraining, in-context world modeling
- stage: accelerating
- confidence: high
- first_observed: 2026-08-29
- last_evidence: 2026-10-27
- evidence:
  - 2026-10-27 — https://deepmind.google/blog/genie-4-a-new-generation-of-world-simulation/ — Google DeepMind official blog, "Genie 4: A new generation of world simulation" (opened this session via the tier-i DeepMind RSS sweep; HN front page 813pts, the day's clear #1 AI story, corroborated by AlphaSignal): DeepMind's newest world model, natively MULTIMODAL — audio (music, ambient sound) and interactive dialogue generated simultaneously with the visual world rather than bolted on after — built on the Gemini family (Gemini Omni anchors real-world appearance/behavior; other Gemini models supply the reasoning that keeps a simulation coherent over time). Users steer an evolving simulation in real time via keyboard/camera controls plus natural-language prompts (change the weather, introduce an object/person) while Gemini monitors and keeps the scene progressing cohesively between inputs. Rolling out as a research preview to trusted testers, then Google AI Ultra/Project Genie subscribers and API/AI Studio early-access partners. The single most authoritative entrant yet on this trend's precise claim — a frontier lab's own flagship world-model generation, distinct from an academic group's method or a well-resourced startup's first model (World Labs' Atlas, Figure's Helix). A 23rd independent group. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (last_evidence 10-21 → 10-27); dropped the oldest evidence line (H3-World, 09-01 — still summarized in this trend's seeding narrative) to hold the 10-cap. NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; a major-lab-flagship reinforcement — not itself the registered mainstreaming gate, which specifically wants a serving-framework/major-lab ADOPTION of an EXISTING group's method or independent benchmark reuse, not a lab's own new model).
  - 2026-10-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26855 — Tianqi Liu, Hanfei Zhao, Qifeng Liang, Hui En Pang, Jiarui Xu, Sayang Mu et al., "Ropedia W0: Scaling Loco-Manipulation World Models across Embodiments with Human Experience" (HF daily papers 10-21, 182up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): the first egocentric loco-manipulation world foundation model spanning HUMANS AND ROBOTS with diverse embodiments jointly — curates Xp-Data (10k hours of egocentric human data + 3k hours of robot data with unified multimodal annotations) and a unified kinematic representation encoding locomotion and manipulation together, addressing the heterogeneous-action-space/limited-multimodal-supervision gap that has kept locomotion and manipulation modeled separately. A 22nd independent group and a concrete, named artifact+dataset squarely on the trend's video/human-experience-pretraining axis, joining the same-week egocentric-world-modeling cluster already queued (EgoXperience, Holi-World, both 10-20). NO stage move (accelerating/HIGH; reinforcement, mainstreaming gate still not fired).
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22364 — Yining Hong, Li Fei-Fei et al. (Stanford/CMU), "T2-z: Test-Time Optimization of Environment Latents for Robotic World Models" (found this session via the alphaXiv curator lane, followed to the arXiv primary; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): at test time the world model stays FROZEN and a newly-initialized environment latent is optimized from one or more test-time interactions, adapting the model to previously-unseen environmental configurations (friction, mass, physical rules) without retraining — across 7 simulated settings and 4 real-world robotic tasks, reduces object-prediction error substantially versus a frozen non-adapting baseline. A 21st independent group and a distinct maturity facet — test-time adaptation of an already-trained world model to novel environments, complementing RoboJEPA's scaling-law facet (10-07) rather than duplicating it. (Meta FAIR), "RoboJEPA: Scaling Robotic Latent World Models" (found this session via the alphaXiv curator lane, followed to the arXiv primary; abs + metadata via export.arxiv): a JEPA-based latent world model trained across 12 robotic embodiments establishing the trend's first PRINCIPLED SCALING LAW — imagination error (the latent-rollout error) follows a second-order power law in compute, letting model quality be predicted well beyond the fitted scale, and downstream planning performance improves predictably with compute. A 20th independent group and a MAJOR non-academic entrant (Meta FAIR) landing exactly the maturity signal this trend has been watching for since W38 — a well-resourced lab establishing quantitative scaling laws (not just another architecture/mechanism) for the video/robot-pretrained world-model substrate. Single new independent primary → EVIDENCE add (backfill, same date as the current last_evidence line below — last_evidence stays 10-07); dropped the oldest evidence line (Agentic Game Development, 08-26 — still summarized in this trend's seeding narrative) to hold the 10-cap.   - 2026-10-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03391 — Zhaochong An, Fei Zhang, Menglin Jia, Duncan Frost, Zijian Zhou, Yikai Wang, Xudong Wang, Aditya Patel, Belinda Zeng, Tao Xiang, Serge Belongie, Amir Bar, Sen He, "NAVA-WAM: Native Action-Prior Learning from Videos for World Action Models" (HF daily-papers exploration slot, 77up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): directly attacks the trend's own core scarcity bottleneck — action-ANNOTATED robot trajectories are expensive/limited — by learning action priors natively from observation-ONLY video, avoiding both prior routes (pretrain-then-adapt visual representations, or infer-then-ground latent actions) that existing WAMs use to extract signal from unlabeled footage. An 18th independent group (overlaps with the same-week "ProWAM" progressive-visual-planning paper by a subset of these authors, but NAVA-WAM's native-action-prior mechanism is the more novel contribution — picked as today's evidence to avoid stacking two same-lab lines).   - 2026-09-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34981 — Renping Zhou, Zanlin Ni, Zihao Fan, Guohao Fu, Zeyu Liu, Hao Shi, "What Makes World Action Models Generalize? An Empirical Study of Test-Time Future Modeling" (HF daily papers, 45up; abs + metadata via export.arxiv this session): a rigorous empirical study directly on this trend's own construction choice — whether a WAM must still generate future frames at inference (Explicit WAMs) or can discard that cost entirely (Latent WAMs) — finding Latent WAMs match Explicit ones on in-distribution tasks but FAIL to retain the generalization benefit that motivated WAMs in the first place, i.e. the field's efficiency shortcut quietly gives up the property the whole approach exists for. A 17th independent group and the trend's first dedicated empirical audit of its own explicit-vs-latent design axis (distinct from PAWBench's distributional-alignment evaluation). A 16th independent group and a SECOND major non-academic entrant (after World Labs' Atlas) — a well-resourced humanoid-robotics company independently landing this trend's exact substrate claim (learned representations from human video/demonstration data transferring to open-ended embodied generalization) at a genuinely new real-world scale (30 unseen homes, not a lab benchmark).
